# Seed 76 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - the stopping count's third run gains nothing; a dominance chain hides its fastest designs (Iteration 555)

`seed 76 10h then experiment review-checks.py spread then experiment appbench.py --zip ~/Downloads/appbench.zip --sample 16` at 989e944c. The chain: done. 36,200 designs, generations 0-141: 22,758 ok, 8,839 stopped after one round, 4,573 screened, 28 wrong output: apps, 2 died: corpus; 3,620 designs an hour.

**The front**: the true one 262 designs - the report's 262, the same.

**The fixed-box hypervolume**: true front 262 (248 inside the box), hypervolume 0.35036 | fastest 0.1450, smallest 14109, least memory 112 KB, fastest at 32 bits 0.1778. **The stopping rule** (stopping.py, results/stopping-series.md): verdict: seed 76 gains nothing - the hypervolume 0.35036 against 0.35667 (-1.8%); the fastest 0.1450 against 0.1363 (+6.4%); the smallest 14,109 against 14,079 (+0.2%); the least memory 112 KB against 112 KB (+0 KB); the fastest at 32 bits 0.1778 against 0.1723 (+3.2%) the count: 3 run(s) on the method; the earliest stop after seed 77, if every run until then gains nothing the next run gains if: the hypervolume >= 0.36024, the fastest <= 0.1336, the smallest <= 13,938, the least memory <= 108 KB, the fastest at 32 bits <= 0.1688

**The extremes**: fastest 7f6362b7e5 0.1450, smallest 2f960a1e7c 14,109 bytes, least memory 112 KB, fastest at 32 bits a011d3e1ee 0.1778.

**One round first**: 8,839 children stopped after one round, 28.1% of those measured; 920 audited in full, 0 of them on the front.

**The operators**: borrowed 40.4%, crossover 25.6%, mutation 34.1% of the children; the last fifth borrowed 42.4%, crossover 24.8%, mutation 32.8%; the front's entries per child 0.727% (262 of 36,061).

**The JIT designs**: 73 of the front, 26 of its fastest tenth's 26; jitmul 43, jitfuse 10, jitexec 1, jitos 19, jitrp 22.

**The fast end re-measured** (remeasure.json, 107 designs): the fastest ten 1.059 re-measured / in-run, the JIT designs 1.043, the rest 0.988.

**The experiments**: the spread at generation 102 (session 63) - seeds 75 and 76 lead the fast end, 0.1384 and 0.1383 against seeds 72-74's 0.1469-0.1520; at 32 bits seed 74 leads (0.1731; seed 76 0.1856); appbench - **Spearman, in-run speed against the applications' geometric mean: 0.894 over 16 designs.**; the clock test - none; truefronts - none.

**The remote checks** (results/xcheck-seed76.md): dream.lan-prebuilt 24 designs measured on both: Spearman 0.972; dream.lan-prebuilt32 30 designs measured on both: Spearman 0.952; dream.lan-prebuilt64 30 designs measured on both: Spearman 0.980; rage.lan-native 16 designs measured on both: Spearman 0.832.

**The stopping rule - seed 76 gains nothing** (stopping.py, its first run on a returned seed): the hypervolume 0.35036
against seed 74's 0.35667 (-1.8%); the fastest 0.1450 against seed 75's 0.1363 (+6.4%); the smallest 14,109 against
14,079; the least memory 112 KB both; the fastest at 32 bits 0.1778 against 0.1723 - none past its threshold.
**Prediction 553 holds.** The count: seed 74 the first, 75 a gain, 76 nothing - a 10-hour seed 77 gaining nothing would
have ended it; the owner chose 12 hours for seed 77, a method change (GOALS.md: the hours are the method's) - a new
series.

**A dominance chain hides the fast end.** 105 designs alive in the records read faster than the front's fastest
(7f6362b7e5, 0.1450) - down to cb23d81d6f at 0.1383, each with the gate's two readings. None is on the front: the
evolver's dominance takes speeds within 2% (SPEED_TOL) and memory within 8 KB (MEM_TOL) as ties, which is not transitive
- each fast design is dominated by one a little slower and smaller, that one by another, up to 7f6362b7e5. The strict
front (hv.py with EVOLVE_SPEED_TOL=0 EVOLVE_MEM_TOL=0): 1,296 designs, the hypervolume 0.36757, the fastest 0.1383, the
least memory 108 KB, the fastest at 32 bits 0.1714. The stopping rule's thresholds - speed 2%, memory 4 KB - sit at
those tolerances, so an extreme can cross its threshold by whether such a chain forms; seed 75's one gain was the
fastest's. Seeds 74-75 cannot be checked for chains here: their records stay on the laptop, their results files hold the
fronts and their re-measured company only. The owner's to decide, for the series seed 77 opens: the extremes from every
alive design (the strict front's), the hypervolume as before.

**The votes** (the true front): all four this run - jitdoes, jitos; over 74-76 **jitdoes the one freeze candidate** (12
of 12, no keep vote) - jitrp and jitret no longer (jitret 11 of 12, with a keep vote). A freeze changes the method: the
owner's; none frozen.

**The rules** (review-checks.py rules): 1 the verdict - the stopping rule's, above (no comparison step); 2 PASS (jitret
11 of 12, a keep vote); 3 ATTENTION (1.063 - seed 75's 1.047 passed); 4 PASS; 5 PASS (28 32-bit failures, every one
cpt16's reach); 6 PASS (14,109); 7 PASS (rtiplus on 22%, working); 8 tail retired - its votes count nothing; 9 PASS (2
designs rely on the rtimm=2 / rtfuse overlap: no change); 10 run-tests and the corpus pass (the chain on master,
Iteration 555).

**Two designs died on the corpus** in the run - e80800407e (generation 67) and d11c9a6300 (85), both cv8 JIT designs, no
error text recorded; neither near the front. Reproduced here (555): neither dies - each prints a corpus output different from s6's, the same every time (7827eeea, f36f87e4 against s6's 5a4c122a; ten others from the records match): a wrong computation in a JIT combination, a death on the laptop - an open correctness issue, off the front. Their genomes and the steps: results/drafts/corpus-deaths-555.md.

**Held out** (heldout.py): sieve's Spearman 0.936, the fastest third 0.853, the fastest tenth 0.901; regret +1.5% - the
selection's fastest 7f6362b7e5 0.069 on sieve, the held-out fastest 2cbc366040 0.068.

**The curse**: the fastest tenth re-measured / in-run 1.063, the fastest third 1.042 (seed 75: 1.047, 1.021); the re-
measure's fastest ten 1.059, the JIT designs 1.043, the rest 0.988 - below 1 again, as a cost every run pays predicts
for the slower designs (results/drafts/fast-end-offset-554.md).

**Progress**: the smallest gained in 24 generations, the last at 135 of 141 (128 bytes in the last third); the best
speed by 2% or more in 12, the last at generation 67.

Facts: the pack's own checks PASS, 7 IDENTICAL, jail PASS; results-measured-here.patch 4 files, not applied; the remote
checks of seed 75's front - Spearman 0.972, 0.952, 0.980 at dream's three builds, 0.832 on the Tegra (16); against the
laptop's 32-bit speeds 0.943 (dream) and 0.972 (the Tegra); 3d4e860a9f, which the laptop rejected, is faster than the
front's fastest on dream at both widths - the fast end named per machine (results/drafts/xcheck-agreement-546.md);
generic - 27764919a0 the one design measured on all three classes (0.471).

---

# Evolved VM designs

31627 designs evaluated, 22758 alive, 4573 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann, apps,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): 8e25841fc5, speed 0.350, binary + image 19,673, memory 140 KB - Q 0.293 of s6-cv8b's.

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.
